### Physics → PHYSICS 11 (C11-PHY)

#### Ch 1 — Measurements
PDF 4–21 · printed 1–18 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 4) with 9 bullets

*Introduces physical quantities, the SI system (base/derived units, prefixes, writing conventions), uncertainty in measurement (absolute, fractional, percentage), significant figures and rounding rules, precision vs accuracy, propagation of uncertainty in results, and dimensional analysis (homogeneity check and derivation of formulas).*

- 1.1 Physical Quantities and Their Units — PDF p.5
  - base vs derived quantities *(unnumbered)*
  - two steps of measuring a base quantity (standard, comparison) *(unnumbered)*
- 1.2 International System of Units — PDF p.5
  - Base Units (Table 1.1, seven base units) *(unnumbered)*
  - Derived Units (Table 1.2; plane angle, solid angle) *(unnumbered)*
  - Supplementary/additional units accepted with SI (degree, litre, tonne, u, eV, nautical mile, bar, angstrom, barn) *(unnumbered)*
  - Scientific Notation *(unnumbered)*
  - Prefixes (Table 1.3) *(unnumbered)*
  - Conventions for Using SI Units (16 numbered rules, PDF 7-8) *(unnumbered)*
- 1.3 Uncertainty in Measurement — PDF p.8
  - absolute, fractional and percentage uncertainty *(unnumbered)*
  - Uncertainty in Digital Instruments *(unnumbered)*
- 1.4 Use of Significant Figures — PDF p.9
  - Working with significant figures: (i) Counting significant digits (rules a-e), (ii) Multiplying or dividing numbers (rounding rules a-c), (iii) In adding or subtracting numbers *(unnumbered)*
  - Limitations of Significant Figures *(unnumbered)*
- 1.5 Precision and Accuracy — PDF p.11
  - precision = least count / absolute uncertainty *(unnumbered)*
  - accuracy = fractional/percentage uncertainty *(unnumbered)*
  - metre rule vs Vernier callipers example *(unnumbered)*
- 1.6 Assessment of Total Uncertainty in the Final Result — PDF p.12
  - 1. For addition and subtraction *(unnumbered)*
  - 2. For multiplication and division *(unnumbered)*
  - 3. For power factor *(unnumbered)*
  - Examples 1.1-1.3 (PDF 14) *(unnumbered)*
- 1.7 Dimensions of Physical Quantities — PDF p.15
  - dimension symbols [L],[M],[T],[A],[theta] *(unnumbered)*
  - Homogeneity of Physical Equations *(unnumbered)*
  - Derivation of a Formula (Example 1.4 centripetal force) *(unnumbered)*
  - Limitations in Dimensional Analysis (PDF 17) *(unnumbered)*

- Visuals: Figure/labelled diagram 5, Flowchart/chart 1, Graph/chart 1, Photo/illustration 2, Table 7 — e.g. C11-PHY/Ch1/Tab1.1, C11-PHY/Ch1/Vis1-1, C11-PHY/Ch1/Tab1.2, C11-PHY/Ch1/Vis1-2
- Exercises: Multiple Choice Questions (16); Short Answer Questions (8); Constructed Response Questions (12); Comprehensive Questions (5); Numerical Problems (8) · answers: No MCQ key. Numerical answers printed in brackets after questions (PDF 21) for 6 of 8 problems.

#### Ch 2 — Force and Motion
PDF 22–53 · printed 19–50 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 22), ~9 bullets with bracketed sub-notes

*Builds vector algebra (components, scalar and vector products), derives the three equations of motion algebraically and graphically, applies them to free fall and projectile motion, then develops linear momentum, impulse, conservation of momentum, elastic and inelastic collisions in one and two dimensions, and rocket propulsion.*

PDF 54–74 · printed 51–71 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 54), 13 bullets

*Develops angular kinematics (radian, angular displacement/velocity/acceleration, relation to linear quantities, angular equations of motion), centripetal force and its applications (centrifuge, banked roads), artificial satellites (critical/orbital velocity, weightlessness, artificial gravity), moment of inertia, angular momentum and its conservation with applications (diver, skater, flywheel, gyroscope).*

#### Ch 4 — Work, Energy and Power
PDF 75–92 · printed 72–89 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 75), 5 bullets

*Defines work by constant and variable forces (area under F-d graph), conservative vs non-conservative forces, power, kinetic energy, absolute gravitational potential energy, escape velocity, the work-energy theorem, and interconversion of PE and KE with and without friction.*

- 4.1 Work Done by a Constant Force — PDF p.75
  - W = Fd (4.1), pushing a wall (Fig 4.1) *(unnumbered)*
  - W = F d cos theta = F.d (4.2, 4.3) (Fig 4.2, 4.3) *(unnumbered)*
  - 1 J = 1 N m *(unnumbered)*
  - force-displacement graph for constant force (Fig 4.4) *(unnumbered)*
  - properties (i)-(v): scalar, positive/zero/negative work, SI unit *(unnumbered)*
- 4.2 Work Done by a Variable Force — PDF p.76
  - path divided into n intervals (Fig 4.5) *(unnumbered)*
  - W_total = sum F_i cos theta_i delta d_i (4.4); limit (4.5) (Fig 4.6, 4.7) *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 4.1 area under F-d graph (Fig 4.8) (PDF 77-78) *(unnumbered)*
- 4.3 Conservative and Non-Conservative Forces — PDF p.78
  - Conservative Forces: gravitational field, paths 1, 2, 3 from A to B (Fig 4.9, 4.10), W = -mgh independent of path *(unnumbered)*
  - definition of conservative force; examples electrostatic, elastic spring *(unnumbered)*
  - Non-Conservative Forces: kinetic friction, air resistance, tension, normal force, rocket propulsion (PDF 79) *(unnumbered)*
- 4.4 Power — PDF p.79
  - P_av = delta W/delta t (4.6); instantaneous P (4.7); P = F.v (4.8) *(unnumbered)*
  - watt; kilowatt-hour = 3.6 x 10^6 J *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 4.2 (PDF 80) *(unnumbered)*
- 4.5 Energy — PDF p.80
  - (i) Kinetic energy, (ii) Potential energy *(unnumbered)*
  - Kinetic Energy: derivation via car braking against friction, KE = 1/2 m v^2 (4.9) (PDF 81) *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 4.3 (PDF 81-82) *(unnumbered)*
  - Potential Energy; elastic potential energy *(unnumbered)*
  - Absolute Potential Energy: steps from point 1 to N (Fig 4.11), Eqs 4.10-4.12, U = -GMm/r, U_g = -GMm/R (4.13) (PDF 82-84) *(unnumbered)*
- 4.6 Escape Velocity — PDF p.85
  - 1/2 m v_esc^2 = GMm/R; v_esc = sqrt(2GM/R) (4.14) = sqrt(2gR) (4.15) ~ 11 km/s *(unnumbered)*
- 4.7 Work-Energy Theorem — PDF p.85
  - a = (vf^2 - vi^2)/2d (4.16); F = ma (4.17); Fd = 1/2 m vf^2 - 1/2 m vi^2 (4.18) *(unnumbered)*
  - statement; applicable for any direction / variable force *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 4.4 motorcycle on 24 deg slope (PDF 86-87) *(unnumbered)*
- 4.8 Interconversion of Potential Energy and Kinetic Energy — PDF p.87

#### Ch 5 — Solids and Fluid Dynamics
PDF 93–118 · printed 90–115 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 93), 19 bullets

*Covers the structure of solids, mechanical properties (stress, strain, Young's modulus, Searle's experiment, stress-strain curve, strain energy), Archimedes' principle and floatation, then fluid dynamics: ideal fluid, rate of flow, equation of continuity, Bernoulli's equation and its applications (aerofoil lift, Magnus effect, filter pump, carburetor, atomizer, Venturi meter, Torricelli's theorem), viscous drag, Stokes' law, terminal velocity, real fluids and superfluids.*

#### Ch 6 — Heat and Thermodynamics
PDF 119–138 · printed 116–135 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 119), ~22 bullets

*Presents the kinetic theory of gases and ideal gas equation (incl. Boltzmann form and gas laws), internal energy, work by a gas (P delta V), the first law of thermodynamics with isothermal and adiabatic processes, reversible/irreversible processes, heat engines, the second law (Kelvin and Clausius statements), Carnot engine/theorem, refrigerators and coefficient of performance, and entropy.*

- 6.1 Assumptions of the Kinetic Theory of Gases — PDF p.120
  - eight assumptions (1-8): constant random motion, negligible volume, no intermolecular forces, elastic collisions, large number, KE proportional to T, pressure due to collisions, negligible collision time *(unnumbered)*
  - Limitations of Kinetic Molecular Theory: Real Gases (PDF 121) *(unnumbered)*
  - Equation of State for an Ideal Gas PV = nRT (6.1) *(unnumbered)*
  - Real gas to Behave Like an Ideal Gas (low pressure, high temperature) *(unnumbered)*
  - Ideal Gas Equation in Terms of Boltzmann Constant: k_B = R/N_A (6.2); PV = N k_B T (6.3) (PDF 122) *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 6.1 *(unnumbered)*
  - Gas Laws: Boyle's Law (Fig 6.1), Charles' Law (Fig 6.2), Gay-lussac's Law (Fig 6.3) (PDF 123-124) *(unnumbered)*
  - Thermal Equilibrium (coffee/spoon example) *(unnumbered)*
- 6.2 Internal Energy — PDF p.124
  - sum of molecular KE and PE; ideal gas internal energy = translational KE *(unnumbered)*
  - <1/2 m v^2> = 3/2 k_B T *(unnumbered)*
- 6.3 Work and Heat — PDF p.125
  - sign convention (work by system positive) *(unnumbered)*
  - gas in cylinder with piston (Fig 6.4 a,b): W = F delta y = P A delta y = P delta V (6.4) *(unnumbered)*
  - area under P-V graph (Fig 6.5) *(unnumbered)*
- 6.4 First Law of Thermodynamics — PDF p.126
  - Q = delta U + W (6.5); statement *(unnumbered)*
  - 1. Conservation Principle, 2. Wider Applicability *(unnumbered)*
  - bicycle pump demonstration (Fig 6.6); human metabolism (PDF 126-127) *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 6.2 *(unnumbered)*
  - Isothermal Process (isotherm, Fig 6.7a): delta U = 0, Q = W (PDF 127-128) *(unnumbered)*
  - Adiabatic Process (adiabat, Fig 6.7b): W = -delta U; PV^gamma = constant; examples (burst tyre, sound wave, cloud formation) *(unnumbered)*
- 6.5 Reversible and Irreversible Processes — PDF p.128
  - reversible process; cycle; reversible cycle *(unnumbered)*
  - irreversible process (explosion, friction, conduction) (PDF 129) *(unnumbered)*
- 6.6 Heat Engine — PDF p.129
  - steam engine origin; working principle (hot reservoir -> work -> cold reservoir) *(unnumbered)*
- 6.7 Second Law of Thermodynamics — PDF p.129
  - engine schematic (Fig 6.8): Q1, W, Q2; W = Q1 - Q2 *(unnumbered)*
  - petrol engines ~25%, diesel 35-40% (PDF 130) *(unnumbered)*
  - Lord Kelvin's statement; need for source and sink *(unnumbered)*
- 6.8 Carnot Engine and Carnot's Theorem — PDF p.130
  - Sadi Carnot 1824; Carnot cycle on PV diagram (Fig 6.9): 4 steps AB isothermal expansion, BC adiabatic expansion, CD isothermal compression, DA adiabatic compression (PDF 130-131) *(unnumbered)*
  - efficiency eta = (Q1 - Q2)/Q1 (6.6) = (T1 - T2)/T1 (6.7); percentage efficiency = (1 - T2/T1) x 100 *(unnumbered)*
  - Carnot's theorem (two statements) (PDF 132) *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 6.3 *(unnumbered)*
- 6.9 Refrigerator — PDF p.132
  - heat engine in reverse (Fig 6.10); Clausius statement; Q2 + W = Q1 *(unnumbered)*
  - Co-efficient of Performance of Refrigerator: E = Q2/W = Q2/(Q1 - Q2) = T2/(T1 - T2) (PDF 133) *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 6.4 *(unnumbered)*
- 6.11 Entropy — PDF p.133
  - (section 6.10 SKIPPED in numbering) *(unnumbered)*
  - Rudolph Clausius 1856; delta S = delta Q/T (6.8) (PDF 134) *(unnumbered)*
  - net entropy increase in natural processes; entropy and molecular disorder; degradation of energy; entropy of universe always increases (PDF 134-135) *(unnumbered)*
  - Example 6.5 entropy change of melting ice *(unnumbered)*

PDF 139–173 · printed 136–170 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 139), 12 bullets

*Introduces wave parameters and types, the principle of superposition and its consequences: interference (coherent sources, path-difference conditions), stationary waves on strings and in organ pipes (harmonics), a microwave standing-wave experiment, diffraction (ripple tank), beats, wave intensity, and the Doppler effect with its applications.*

  - same phase: y = y1 + y2; opposite phase: y = y1 - y2 (Fig 7.4 a,b) (PDF 142) *(unnumbered)*

  - phenomena: (i) interference, (ii) beats, (iii) stationary waves *(unnumbered)*

  - Case (2): organ pipe closed at one end (Fig 7.9): (a) fundamental lambda1 = 4l (7.10), f1 = v/4l; (b) second mode lambda3 = 4l/3 (7.11), f3 = 3 f1; (c) nth mode lambda_n = 4l/n, n odd (7.12) (PDF 152-153) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 9 — Electrostatics and Current Electricity
PDF 192–228 · printed 189–225 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 192), ~22 bullets

*Develops electrostatics (quantization of charge, Coulomb's law, electric field and field lines, electric flux, Gauss's law, electric potential and potential gradient, electron volt, motion of charges in uniform fields, Faraday cage) and current electricity (current, drift velocity, I = nAvq, Ohm's law, resistivity and temperature coefficient, power, emf and internal resistance, Kirchhoff's rules, Wheatstone bridge, potentiometer, null method, thermistors, LDRs and carbon-fibre bridge monitoring).*

- Quantization of charge q = Ne; electrostatics *(unnumbered)* — PDF p.192

#### Ch 11 — Special Theory of Relativity
PDF 251–260 · printed 248–257 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 251), 6 bullets

*Introduces relative motion and frames of reference (inertial/non-inertial), the two postulates of special relativity and their consequences (relativity of simultaneity, time dilation, length contraction, relativistic mass), mass-energy equivalence E = mc^2, and the concept of curved space-time as Einstein's picture of gravity.*

#### Ch 12 — Nuclear and Particle Physics
PDF 261–279 · printed 258–276 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Learning Objectives' box on opener (PDF 261), ~20 bullets

*Covers nuclear structure (nucleons, atomic/mass numbers, isotopes), the four fundamental forces, matter and antimatter (pair production and annihilation), radioactivity (alpha, beta-minus, beta-plus, gamma decay, energy spectra), fundamental particles (fermions/bosons, hadrons/leptons), quarks and colour, beta decay in terms of quarks, the Higgs boson, conservation laws, matter-antimatter asymmetry, plasma as dominant state of matter, quantum field theory and string theory, and the Standard Model.*

#### Ch 3 — Chemical Bonding
PDF 46–73 · printed 46–73 · SLOs [C-11-A-26 to C-11-A-46] printed p46; 21 SLOs

*Lewis view of ionic, covalent and dative bonds and expanded octets, bond polarity and dipole moments from electronegativity, then the three bonding models: VSEPR (AB2-AB6 shapes, drug-design application), valence bond theory with sigma/pi overlap and sp3/sp2/sp hybridization, and molecular orbital theory with MO diagrams and bond orders of He2, N2, O2. Ends with bond energy/length data and a comparison of bond strengths with intermolecular forces.*

#### Ch 8 — Chemical Equilibrium
PDF 167–188 · printed 167–188 · SLOs [C-11-A-135 to C-11-A-148] printed p167; 14 SLOs

*Reversible reactions reaching dynamic equilibrium, interpreted through microscopic and macroscopic events, including physical (liquid-vapour) equilibria. ### Biology → Biology 11 (C11-BIO)

#### Ch 1 — Biodiversity and Classification
PDF 4–35 · printed 1–32 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; 'Students' Learning Outcomes' list of 9 bullets (PDF 4)

*Introduces the three-domain system (Woese) replacing Whittaker's five kingdoms, the taxonomic hierarchy, and surveys the kingdoms of Eukarya, the major animal phyla and vertebrate classes, virus classification (ICTV criteria), biodiversity assessment (levels, random sampling, quadrat/transect/aerial, mark-recapture) and species/speciation modes.*

- 1.1 Three-Domain System of Classification — PDF p.4
  - Five-kingdom system (Whittaker 1969) vs three domains (Woese 1990) *(unnumbered)*
  - Domain Archaea (unnumbered, PDF 5): membrane ether-linked branched lipids, cell wall without peptidoglycan, genetic differences, methanogenesis; major groups Methanogens, Halobacteria, Thermococci, Thaumarchaeota (PDF 6); side box 'Significance of Archaea' *(unnumbered)*
  - Domain Bacteria (unnumbered, PDF 6-8): 14 general characteristics (peptidoglycan wall, plasmids, binary fission, shapes cocci/bacilli/spirilla/vibrios, arrangements, flagella, pili, respiration types, extremophiles, pathogenicity, symbiosis); major groups Proteobacteria, Firmicutes, Actinobacteria, Cyanobacteria, Spirochaetes, Acidobacteria, Aquificae *(unnumbered)*
  - Domain Eukarya (unnumbered, PDF 8): 6 characteristics incl. endosymbiosis (numbering skips 4) *(unnumbered)*
- 1.2 Taxonomic Hierarchy — PDF p.8
  - Taxa: Domain, Kingdom, Phylum, Class, Order, Family, Genus, Species with examples (PDF 9-10) *(unnumbered)*
  - Uncaptioned table: ranks of Human, Sparrow, Onion (PDF 10) *(unnumbered)*
- 1.3 Salient Features of Kingdoms of Domain Eukarya — PDF p.10
  - 1. Kingdom Protista: Protozoa, Algae, Myxomycota/Oomycota (PDF 10) *(unnumbered)*
  - 2. Kingdom Fungi: Zygomycota, Ascomycota, Basidiomycota (PDF 11) *(unnumbered)*
  - 3. Kingdom Plantae: nonvascular (bryophytes), vascular seedless/seed plants (PDF 11-12) *(unnumbered)*
  - Table: Distinguishing characteristics of the kingdoms of three domains (PDF 13) *(unnumbered)*
  - 4. Kingdom Animalia (PDF 13) *(unnumbered)*
- 1.4 Classification of Kingdom Animalia — PDF p.13
  - 1- Phylum Porifera (PDF 13): ostia, osculum, pinacocytes, choanocytes, gemmules *(unnumbered)*
  - 2- Phylum Cnidaria (PDF 14-15): diploblastic, cnidocytes/nematocyst, polyp and medusa *(unnumbered)*
  - 3- Phylum Platyhelminthes (PDF 15): acoelomate, flame cells, protonephridia *(unnumbered)*
  - 4- Phylum Nematoda (PDF 16): pseudocoelom, Aschelminths box *(unnumbered)*
  - 5- Phylum Mollusca (PDF 17): mantle, radula, open circulation, nephridia *(unnumbered)*
  - 6- Phylum Annelida (PDF 18-19): metameric segmentation, setae, metanephridia, closed circulation *(unnumbered)*
  - 7- Phylum Arthropoda (PDF 19-20): jointed appendages, tagmata, ecdysis, haemolymph, trachea, Malpighian tubules *(unnumbered)*
  - 8- Phylum Echinodermata (PDF 20-21): water-vascular system, madreporite, tube feet *(unnumbered)*
  - 10- Phylum Chordata (PDF 21-22; numbering skips 9): notochord, pharyngeal slits, dorsal tubular nerve cord, post-anal tail; Subphylum Urochordata, Cephalochordata *(unnumbered)*

  - Group Pisces (3 classes) and Group Tetrapoda (4 classes) *(unnumbered)*
  - 1. Cyclostomata, 2. Chondrichthyes, 3. Osteichthyes (PDF 23-24) *(unnumbered)*
  - 4. Amphibia (PDF 24-25), 5. Reptilia - amniotic egg (PDF 25-26), 6. Aves - feathers, keel, crop, gizzard, syrinx, air sacs (PDF 26-27) *(unnumbered)*
  - 7. Mammalia - egg-laying, marsupials (pouched), placental/eutherians (PDF 27-28) *(unnumbered)*

  - Acellular nature, ICTV guidelines; prions and viroids box *(unnumbered)*
  - Classification by host range, morphology (helical, icosahedral, complex, enveloped, naked), genetic material (dsDNA/ssDNA/ssRNA/dsRNA), replication strategy (+sense, -sense, reverse transcribing) (PDF 29-30) *(unnumbered)*

  - Definition; Ecosystem and Niche definitions box *(unnumbered)*
  - Biodiversity assessment levels: species, genetic, ecosystem *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 2 — Bacteria and Viruses
PDF 36–50 · printed 33–47 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; Students' Learning Outcomes list of 11 bullets (PDF 36)

*Describes the prokaryotic bacterial cell (wall, Gram +/- differences, membrane, mesosomes, nucleoid, plasmids), endospore formation, modes of motility and flagellar structure/arrangements, the major ecological groups of bacteria, their beneficial roles and normal flora, and the structure of viruses with T4 bacteriophage and HIV as models.*

  - Cytoplasm and Genetic Material (unnumbered, PDF 38): 70S ribosomes (50S+30S), nucleoid, plasmids (vectors box) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 3 — Cells and Subcellular Organelles
PDF 51–93 · printed 48–90 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; Students' Learning Outcomes list of 15 bullets (PDF 51)

*Covers cell theory and microscopy, then the ultrastructure and function of every cell component (wall, fluid-mosaic membrane, nucleus, ER, ribosomes, mitochondria, chloroplasts, Golgi, lysosomes, peroxisomes, glyoxysomes, vacuoles, centrioles, cytoskeleton, cilia/flagella), contrasts prokaryotic and eukaryotic cells, and explains cell signalling, membrane transport (passive, active, bulk) and stem cells.*

  - Seven properties of life performed by cells: movement, nutrition, respiration, excretion, homeostasis, growth, reproduction (PDF 51-52) *(unnumbered)*
- 3.2 Cell Theory — PDF p.52
  - History: Hooke 1665, Leeuwenhoek 1673, Lamarck 1809, Brown 1831, Schleiden 1838, Schwann 1839, Virchow 1855 (printed '1885'), Pasteur 1862 (PDF 52-53) *(unnumbered)*
  - Three essential points of cell theory *(unnumbered)*
  - Validation of Cell Theory (unnumbered, 4 points, PDF 53) *(unnumbered)*
  - Exceptions to Cell Theory (unnumbered, 4 points, PDF 53-54) *(unnumbered)*

  - Light Microscopy (unnumbered, PDF 54-55); Magnification and Resolution box *(unnumbered)*
  - Electron Microscopy (unnumbered, PDF 55): TEM (250,000x), SEM (100,000x) *(unnumbered)*
  - Table: Difference Between Electron Microscope and Light Microscope (PDF 57) *(unnumbered)*

  - Cell Wall (unnumbered, PDF 57-59): protoplast, primary wall (cellulose, hemicellulose, pectin), middle lamella, secondary wall (lignin), plasmodesmata, functions; cell walls of algae/fungi/prokaryotes/archaea box *(unnumbered)*
  - Plasma Membrane (unnumbered, PDF 59-62): fluid mosaic model (Singer & Nicolson 1972), phospholipid bilayer, cholesterol, membrane protein roles, glycocalyx, functions, 8 techniques to study membrane; table percent by weight of membranes (PDF 61) *(unnumbered)*
  - Cytoplasm and Organelles (unnumbered, PDF 62): cytosol *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 5 — Enzymes
PDF 130–142 · printed 127–139 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; Students' Learning Outcomes list of 14 bullets (PDF 130)

*Explains enzymes as protein biocatalysts with specific active sites, their cofactors/coenzymes (NAD+), the lock-and-key and induced-fit models, how temperature, pH and enzyme/substrate concentration affect reaction rate, inhibition (competitive, non-competitive, reversible, irreversible, feedback) and the two classification schemes.*

- 5.1 Enzymes — PDF p.130
  - Definition, biocatalysts, 10^3-10^8 rate increase; location of enzymes (PDF 130-131) *(unnumbered)*
  - Active Site of Enzyme (unnumbered, PDF 131): binding site and catalytic site; pepsinogen box *(unnumbered)*

  - Activation energy; ES complex; E + S -> ES -> E + P (PDF 132-133) *(unnumbered)*

  - 1. Temperature (optimum 37 C; denaturation; thermophilic bacteria 70 C box) (PDF 134-135) *(unnumbered)*
  - 2. pH (pepsin, trypsin, papain; Table: Optimum pH of important human enzymes) (PDF 135) *(unnumbered)*
  - 3. Enzyme Concentration (PDF 135-136) *(unnumbered)*
  - 4. Substrate Concentration (PDF 136) *(unnumbered)*

  - (heading numbering skips 5.5) Inhibitor definition; aspirin/cyanide box *(unnumbered)*

  - Reversible and Irreversible Inhibitors (penicillin; malonate) (PDF 138) *(unnumbered)*
  - Significance of Enzyme inhibition (5 points) (PDF 138) *(unnumbered)*

  - Classification on the Basis of Reactions: 1- Oxidoreductases, 2- Transferases, 3- Hydrolases, 4- Lyases, 5- Isomerases, 6- Ligases (PDF 139-140) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 6 — Bioenergetics
PDF 143–169 · printed 140–166 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; Students' Learning Outcomes list of 21 bullets (PDF 143)

*Covers how cells capture and release energy: ATP structure and cycle; photosynthesis (pigments, photosystems, light-dependent non-cyclic/cyclic photophosphorylation with chemiosmosis, Calvin cycle); cellular respiration (anaerobic fermentations; aerobic glycolysis, pyruvate oxidation, Krebs cycle, electron transport chain and chemiosmosis, 36 ATP yield); and photorespiration with the C-4 and CAM adaptations.*

- Bioenergetics and ATP: The Energy Currency of Cells (unnumbered) *(unnumbered)* — PDF p.143
  - Definition of bioenergetics (PDF 143) *(unnumbered)*
  - ATP structure (adenine, ribose, 3 phosphates), 7.3 kcal/mole, ATP/ADP/AMP; Lohmann 1929, Lipmann 1941 box (PDF 144) *(unnumbered)*
- 5.1 [=6.1] Photosynthesis (printed as 5.1) — PDF p.145
  - Overall equation; Compensation Point box; ATP-ADP cycle figure (PDF 145) *(unnumbered)*
  - Role of Light: Action Spectrum (Engelmann 1883, Spirogyra) (PDF 145-146) *(unnumbered)*
  - Role of Carbon Dioxide; Role of Water (Van Niel 1930s; 18O isotope experiment 1940s; NADP/NADPH) (PDF 146-147) *(unnumbered)*
  - Role of Photosynthetic Pigments: Chlorophylls (porphyrin ring, Mg, phytol tail; chl a CH3 vs chl b CHO), Accessory Pigments (carotenoids, phycobilins), Absorption Spectrum (430 nm, 670 nm) (PDF 147-149) *(unnumbered)*
  - Organization of Photosynthetic Pigments (Photosystems): antenna complex, reaction centre, PS-I P700, PS-II P680 (PDF 149-150) *(unnumbered)*
  - Mechanism of Photosynthesis: redox; light-dependent (grana) vs light-independent (stroma) (PDF 150) *(unnumbered)*
  - 1- Light-Dependent Reactions: a) Non-Cyclic Photophosphorylation (5 steps: absorption by PS-II, photolysis, electron flow PS-II to PS-I via PQ/cytochrome/PC, absorption by PS-I, electron flow to NADP+ via ferredoxin; Z-scheme), b) Cyclic Photophosphorylation, Chemiosmosis (ATP synthase) (PDF 151-153) *(unnumbered)*
  - 2- Light-Independent Reactions (Calvin cycle; Melvin Calvin Nobel 1961): Phase I Carbon Fixation (Rubisco, RuBP, 3-PGA), Phase II Reduction (G3P), Phase III Regeneration of RuBP (PDF 154-155) *(unnumbered)*
- 5.2 [=6.2] Cellular Respiration (printed as 5.2) — PDF p.155
  - Equation; respiratory fuel; anaerobic vs aerobic (PDF 155) *(unnumbered)*
  - Mechanism of Anaerobic Respiration: a- Alcoholic Fermentation, b- Lactic acid Fermentation; 2 ATP = 14.6 kcal (PDF 156) *(unnumbered)*
  - Mechanism of Aerobic Respiration: four stages (PDF 157) *(unnumbered)*
  - Stage 1: Glycolysis - Preparatory Phase (4 steps), Oxidative Phase (5 steps) (PDF 157-159) *(unnumbered)*
  - Stage 2: Pyruvic acid Oxidation (3 steps; acetyl-CoA) (PDF 159) *(unnumbered)*
  - Stage 3: Krebs Cycle (7 steps; citric acid, alpha-ketoglutaric, succinyl-CoA, succinic, fumaric, malic, oxaloacetic acids; FAD/FADH2) (PDF 159-161) *(unnumbered)*
  - Stage 4: Electron Transport Chain and Chemiosmosis: Pathway of electrons (NADH -> CoQ -> cyt b -> cyt c -> cyt a complex -> O2), Synthesis of ATP (3 ATP per NADH, 2 per FADH2) (PDF 161-162) *(unnumbered)*
  - Substrate-level Phosphorylation (PEP -> pyruvic acid) (PDF 162-163) *(unnumbered)*
  - Overview of the energy extracted from the Oxidation of Glucose (36 ATP net; 18x more efficient) (PDF 163-164) *(unnumbered)*
  - Other Organic Molecules as fuel for Cellular Respiration (proteins, lipids) (PDF 164) *(unnumbered)*
- 5.3 [=6.3] Photorespiration (printed as 5.3) — PDF p.164
  - Definition; Mechanism of Photorespiration (rubisco oxygenase; phosphoglycolate -> glycolate -> glyoxylate -> glycine -> serine -> glycerate across chloroplast, peroxisome, mitochondrion) (PDF 164-165) *(unnumbered)*
  - Disadvantages of Photorespiration (25-50% carbon loss; >28 C) (PDF 165) *(unnumbered)*
  - Adaptations to the problems of Photorespiration: i. C-4 Photosynthesis (PEP, oxaloacetic acid, malic acid, bundle-sheath cells), CAM Metabolism (PDF 165-166) *(unnumbered)*
  - Activity: build a comparison paragraph from photosynthesis/respiration flowchart (PDF 167) *(unnumbered)*

*Surveys plant physiology: mineral nutrition and insectivorous plants, stomatal gas exchange and its mechanisms, support tissues, water potential, water uptake and ascent (TACT), phloem translocation (pressure-flow), meristems and primary/secondary growth with annual rings, plant hormones, osmoregulation in different habitats, thermoregulation, tropisms, photoperiodism (phytochrome) and vernalisation.*

#### Ch 9 — Human Digestive System
PDF 205–217 · printed 202–214 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; Students' Learning Outcomes list of 13 bullets (PDF 205)

*Traces food through the human GI tract - oral cavity (mastication, saliva, salivary amylase), pharynx and swallowing, oesophagus, peristalsis/segmentation, stomach (gastric glands, HCl, pepsin, gastrin), small intestine (pancreatic juice, bile, intestinal juice, villi absorption), large intestine and egestion - and covers liver, gallbladder, pancreas and hormonal control (secretin, CCK).*

- 9.1 Anatomy & Physiology of Digestive System — PDF p.205
  - GI tract and accessory organs (PDF 205) *(unnumbered)*
  - Oral Cavity: selection of food, mechanical digestion (mastication), chemical digestion (salivary glands - sublingual, submaxillary, parotid; saliva pH 8 to 6; salivary amylase; bolus) (PDF 206-207) *(unnumbered)*
  - Pharynx: Swallowing of food (soft palate, epiglottis) (PDF 207-208) *(unnumbered)*
  - Oesophagus (25 cm; cardiac sphincter) (PDF 208) *(unnumbered)*
  - Motility of Alimentary Canal: Peristalsis, Segmentation, Antiperistalsis (vomiting), hunger contractions (PDF 208-209) *(unnumbered)*
  - The Stomach: cardiac, fundus, pyloric portions; mechanical digestion (three muscle layers); chemical digestion (gastric glands - mucous, parietal/oxyntic, chief cells; HCl pH 2-3; pepsinogen to pepsin; chyme; pyloric sphincter); heartburn and rennin boxes (PDF 209-210) *(unnumbered)*
  - Regulation of Secretion of Gastric Juice: nervous (medulla), gastrin, somatostatin (PDF 210-211) *(unnumbered)*
  - The Small Intestine (6 m): Duodenum (pancreatic juice enzymes - amylase, trypsinogen/enterokinase, chymotrypsin, carboxypeptidase, lipase, nucleases; bile; jaundice box), Jejunum and Ileum (intestinal juice enzymes; chyle) (PDF 211-212) *(unnumbered)*
  - Absorption of Digested Food and Water: villi, lacteal, microvilli; sugars/amino acids via hepatic portal vein; fatty acids/glycerol via chylomicrons and lacteals (PDF 212-213) *(unnumbered)*
  - The Large Intestine: Cecum (vermiform appendix; appendicitis box), Colon, Rectum (anal sphincters, defecation reflex; E. coli vitamin K) (PDF 213-214) *(unnumbered)*
  - Control of Egestion (PDF 214) *(unnumbered)*

  - Hormonal Control of the Secretions of Pancreas and Liver: secretin, cholecystokinin (PDF 215) *(unnumbered)*
  - Storage and Metabolic Role of the Liver (PDF 215) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 10 — Human Respiratory System
PDF 218–233 · printed 215–230 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; Students' Learning Outcomes list of 7 bullets (PDF 218)

*Describes external respiration in humans: properties of respiratory surfaces, upper and lower respiratory tracts down to alveoli, breathing mechanics and its medullary control, transport of oxygen (haemoglobin saturation, Bohr effect) and carbon dioxide (bicarbonate/chloride shift, carbaminohaemoglobin, plasma), respiratory pigments (haemoglobin vs myoglobin), and respiratory disorders.*

  - Recalling box; external respiration (PDF 218) *(unnumbered)*
  - Properties of the Respiratory Surface (7 properties) (PDF 219) *(unnumbered)*

  - Lower Respiratory Track - 1- Trachea, 2- Bronchi, Bronchioles, and Alveoli, 3- Lungs (pleura, lobes, alveoli, surfactant box) (PDF 221-223) *(unnumbered)*
  - Mechanism of Breathing or Ventilation: Inspiration, Expiration (diaphragm, intercostal muscles); high altitude box; bird air sacs box (PDF 223-224) *(unnumbered)*
  - Control of Breathing (medulla oblongata) (PDF 224) *(unnumbered)*
- 10.2 Transport of Gases — PDF p.224
  - Transport of Oxygen: plasma 3 mL/L vs whole blood 200 mL/L; PO2 105 mm Hg alveoli; 97% vs 75% saturation; 22% unloaded at rest (PDF 224-225) *(unnumbered)*
  - Factors affecting Oxygen Transport: exercise (62% unloading), Bohr effect, temperature (PDF 225) *(unnumbered)*
  - Transport of Carbon dioxide: 1- As bicarbonate ions (72%; carbonic anhydrase; chloride shift/Hamburger phenomenon), 2- As Carboxyhaemoglobin (20%), 3- As dissolved CO2 in plasma (8%) (PDF 225-226) *(unnumbered)*
  - Carbon Monoxide Poisoning box (PDF 226) *(unnumbered)*
- 10.3 Respiratory Pigments — PDF p.227
  - Haemoglobin: 2 alpha (141 aa) + 2 beta (146 aa) chains, 4 haem groups, 4 O2 (PDF 227) *(unnumbered)*
  - Myoglobin: single chain 153 aa, one haem, O2 storage, releases below 20 mm Hg (PDF 227) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 12 — Human Skeletal and Muscular Systems
PDF 258–282 · printed 255–279 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; Students' Learning Outcomes list of 11 bullets (PDF 258)

*Covers bone (Haversian system, marrow, bone cells, osteogenesis) and cartilage (types), the 206-bone human skeleton (axial and appendicular), joints, skeletal disorders and joint injuries with first aid, the three muscle types, skeletal muscle ultrastructure and myofilament biochemistry, the sliding filament/cross-bridge mechanism, antagonistic muscle action, and muscle disorders.*

  - Bone Marrow: red, yellow (PDF 259-260) *(unnumbered)*
  - Types of Bone Cells: osteoblasts, osteocytes, osteoclasts (PDF 260) *(unnumbered)*
  - Bone Development: osteogenesis, ossification (endochondral) and direct (intramembranous) (PDF 260-261) *(unnumbered)*
  - Structure of Cartilage: perichondrium, matrix, chondrocytes in lacunae; Cartilage Types: hyaline, fibrocartilage, elastic (PDF 261-262) *(unnumbered)*
  - Table: Comparison between Bone and Cartilage (PDF 262) *(unnumbered)*
  - Arrangement of Bones in Skeleton (206 bones): Axial Skeleton - a- Skull (22: 8 cranial, 14 facial), b- Middle ear (6 ossicles), c- Neck bone (hyoid), d- Vertebral column (33: 7 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 5 sacral, 4 coccygeal), e- Rib cage and chest bone (24 ribs: true, false, floating; sternum) (PDF 262-265) *(unnumbered)*
  - Appendicular skeleton: a- Pectoral girdle, b- Forelimbs (30 bones each), c- Pelvic girdle, d- Hindlimbs (30 bones each) (PDF 265-266) *(unnumbered)*
  - Joints: 1- Fibrous, 2- Cartilaginous, 3- Synovial (six types: ball-and-socket, hinge, pivot, ellipsoidal, saddle, gliding); Joint Transplantation box (PDF 266-267) *(unnumbered)*
  - Boxes: Human Skeleton & Musculature helps in Bipedal Posture (7 points); Problems due to Improper Posture (PDF 268) *(unnumbered)*
- 12.2 Disorders of Skeletal System — PDF p.269
  - Disorders of the Skeleton: 1- Disc Slip, 2- Spondylosis, 3- Sciatica, 4- Arthritis (osteoarthritis, rheumatoid, gouty), 5- Osteoporosis (PDF 269-271) *(unnumbered)*

PDF 39–48 · printed 34–43 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION paragraph (PDF 39)

#### Ch 5 — Partial Fractions
PDF 83–92 · printed 78–87 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION paragraph (PDF 83)

*Reverses the addition of rational fractions: a proper rational fraction P(x)/Q(x) is resolved into partial fractions according to the factors of Q(x) - non-repeated linear (Case I), repeated linear (Case II), non-repeated irreducible quadratic (Case III) and repeated irreducible quadratic (Case IV) - by equating coefficients or substituting values in an identity. Improper fractions are first divided.*

  - Conditional equation vs Identity (unnumbered, PDF 83) *(unnumbered)*
  - 5.1.1 Proper Rational Fraction (PDF 84) *(unnumbered)*
  - 5.1.2 Improper Rational Fraction (PDF 84): reduce by long division to polynomial + proper fraction *(unnumbered)*
  - theorem on equal polynomials (coefficients of like powers equal) (PDF 85) *(unnumbered)*
  - 5.1.3 Resolution of a Rational Fraction P(x)/Q(x) into Partial Fractions (PDF 85): steps (i)-(v) *(unnumbered)*
  - Case I: Q(x) has only non-repeated linear factors (PDF 85-87) *(unnumbered)*
  - Case II: Q(x) has repeated linear factors (PDF 88-89) *(unnumbered)*
  - Case III: Q(x) contains non-repeated irreducible quadratic factors (PDF 90-91) *(unnumbered)*
  - Case IV: Q(x) has repeated irreducible quadratic factors (PDF 91-92) *(unnumbered)*

- Key formulas/laws: If two polynomials are equal for all values of the variable they have the same degree and equal coefficients of like powers (PDF 85); Case I: P/Q = A1/(x-a1) + ... + An/(x-an) (PDF 85); Case II: P/Q = A1/(x-a) + A2/(x-a)^2 + ... + An/(x-a)^n (PDF 88); Case III: (Ax+B)/(ax^2+bx+c) (PDF 90); Case IV: (A1x+B1)/(ax^2+bx+c) + ... + (Anx+Bn)/(ax^2+bx+c)^n (PDF 91)

#### Ch 7 — Permutation and Combination
PDF 128–144 · printed 123–139 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION with application bullets and History box (PDF 128)

*Develops counting: the fundamental principle of counting (tree diagrams, product rule with proof), factorial notation, permutations nPr with proof, permutations of objects not all different, circular permutations (two cases), combinations nCr with proof and corollaries, complementary combinations, and applications to cryptography/passwords, lottery odds and selections.*

- 7.2 Permutation — PDF p.131

  - Theorem: nPr = n(n-1)...(n-r+1) = n!/(n-r)! with proof (PDF 132) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 8 — Mathematical Induction and Binomial Theorem
PDF 145–174 · printed 140–169 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION on Maurolico, counter-examples (S(n)=n^2-n+41) and sums of odd numbers (PDF 145-146). TOC lists this unit as 'Binomial Theorem' only.

*Establishes the principle of mathematical induction (base case, induction hypothesis, conclusion) and its extended form, then proves and applies the binomial theorem for positive integral index (general term, middle term, deductions on coefficient sums), extends it to negative/fractional indices (binomial series, approximations, summation of series), builds Pascal's triangle and Pascal's rule, and applies all this to remainders, last digits, divisibility and real-life models.*

- 8.1 Principle of Mathematical Induction — PDF p.146
  - Hypothesis; Induction of Hypothesis (PDF 146) *(unnumbered)*
  - Base Case / Induction of Hypothesis / Conclusion; Procedure for Induction of Hypothesis (PDF 146-147) *(unnumbered)*
  - 8.1.1 Principle of Extended Mathematical Induction (PDF 151): S(n) for n >= i *(unnumbered)*
  - 8.1.2 Real Life Application of Mathematical Induction (PDF 152-153): savings plan, push-ups, calorie intake *(unnumbered)*
- 8.2 Binomial Theorem — PDF p.155
  - binomial, binomial expansion, statement (A), sigma form; Proof by induction (PDF 155-156); Note: binomial coefficients (PDF 156) *(unnumbered)*
  - observations (i)-(vi) on expansion of (a+x)^n; general term T_(r+1) (PDF 157) *(unnumbered)*
  - 8.2.1 The Middle Term in the Expansion of (a+x)^n (PDF 159): Case I n even, Case II n odd *(unnumbered)*
  - 8.2.2 Some Deductions from the binomial expansion of (a+x)^n (PDF 160-161): (1+x)^n, (1-x)^n, sum of coefficients = 2^n, alternating sum = 0, odd = even coefficients = 2^(n-1) *(unnumbered)*
- 8.3 The Binomial Theorem When the Index n is a Negative Integer or a Fraction — PDF p.162
  - binomial series, valid for |x|<1; Note box: proof beyond scope, general term (PDF 162-163) *(unnumbered)*
  - Some particular cases of the expansion of (1+x)^n, n<0 box (i)-(vi) (PDF 163) *(unnumbered)*
  - 8.3.1 Application of the Binomial Theorem (PDF 163): Approximations; Summation of infinite series (PDF 163-166) *(unnumbered)*
- 8.4 Binomial Coefficients Using Pascal's Triangle — PDF p.168
  - construction rules 1-3; Pascal's Triangle figure rows 0-7; Pascal's Rule C(n,k) = C(n-1,k-1) + C(n-1,k) (PDF 168) *(unnumbered)*
- 8.5 Application of Binomial Theorem — PDF p.169
  - 8.5.1 Finding the Remainder using Binomial Theorem (PDF 169): 8^100 mod 7, 2^100 mod 3 *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 9 — Division of Polynomials
PDF 175–181 · printed 170–176 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION paragraph (PDF 175). Running head on every page reads 'Remainder and Factor Theorem' while the opener and TOC say 'Division of Polynomials'.

*Reviews polynomial functions and long division, then states and proves the Remainder Theorem and Factor Theorem, introduces synthetic division as a shortcut for dividing by x - a, and applies these to polynomial regression error, digital signal processing and error detection.*

  - degree, long division (Examples 1-2, PDF 175-176) *(unnumbered)*
  - 9.1.1 Remainder Theorem: statement and proof (PDF 176) *(unnumbered)*
  - 9.1.2 Factor Theorem: statement and proof (PDF 177); Note box *(unnumbered)*

- 9.2 Real Life Applications of Remainder and Factor Theorems — PDF p.179
  - Polynomial Regression (unnumbered, PDF 180): percentage error *(unnumbered)*
  - Digital Signal Processing (DSP) (unnumbered, PDF 180): zero remainder = no error *(unnumbered)*
  - coding theory / error detection (Exercise 9.2 Q8) *(unnumbered)*

- Key formulas/laws: Remainder Theorem: f(x) divided by x - a leaves remainder f(a) (proof, PDF 176); Factor Theorem: x - a is a factor of f(x) iff f(a) = 0 (proof, PDF 177); f(x) = (x-a) q(x) + R, R = f(a) (PDF 176); Error = Observed - Predicted; Percentage Error = |Error/Observed| x 100 (PDF 180)

#### Ch 10 — Trigonometric Identities
PDF 182–204 · printed 177–199 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; short INTRODUCTION (PDF 182)

*Starting from the distance formula, proves the fundamental law of trigonometry cos(a-b) = cos a cos b + sin a sin b on the unit circle and deduces the sum/difference identities, allied-angle identities (with quadrant signs), double-, half- and triple-angle identities, and the product-to-sum and sum-to-product identities, each followed by proof-type exercises.*

  - Note + rules 1-3: odd multiples change ratio to co-ratio; even multiples keep; sign by quadrant (PDF 187) *(unnumbered)*
  - table Measure of the angle / Quad., sign-quadrant figure (PDF 188) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 11 — Trigonometric Functions and their Graphs
PDF 205–222 · printed 200–217 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION paragraph (PDF 205)

#### Ch 12 — Limit and Continuity
PDF 223–241 · printed 218–236 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION paragraph (PDF 223). Opener title 'Limit and Continuity'; TOC 'Limits and Continuity'; running head 'Limit of Sequences and Continuity'.

*Builds the idea of a limit (x approaches zero / infinity / a), estimates limits geometrically (inscribed polygons approaching pi) and numerically (tables), states the limit theorems, evaluates standard limits ((x^n - a^n)/(x-a), rational/radical forms, limits at infinity, limits of sequences, (1+1/n)^n = e, (a^x - 1)/x = ln a, sin x/x = 1 via the sandwich theorem), then treats one-sided limits, continuity at a point and real-world limit models.*

#### Ch 13 — Differentiation
PDF 242–263 · printed 237–258 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION (Newton and Leibniz history, PDF 242)

*Introduces the derivative geometrically (secant lines tending to the tangent) and physically (average to instantaneous velocity), defines it as the limit of a difference quotient, finds derivatives from first principles, proves the power rule for integer n, relates differentiability and continuity (|x| at 0), proves the constant, constant-multiple, sum/difference, product and quotient rules, and applies derivatives to marginal profit, motion, minimum construction cost, investment growth and structural stress.*

- 13.1 Tangent to a Curve at a Point — PDF p.242
  - secant PQ, slope m_sec = (f(x+dx) - f(x))/dx (Figure 13.1); successive secants PQ1, PQ2 ... (Figure 13.2); m_tan as limit (PDF 242-243) *(unnumbered)*
- 13.2 Derivative as the Limit of a Difference Quotient — PDF p.243
  - average rate of change, instantaneous rate of change f'(x), differentiation (PDF 243-244) *(unnumbered)*
  - 13.2.1 Derivative as the rate of change of velocity (PDF 244-246): average velocity tables and graphs for s(t) = t^2 + t, instantaneous velocity *(unnumbered)*
- 13.3 Process of Finding Derivative f'(x) by Definition — PDF p.246
  - 13.3.1 Notation of Derivative (PDF 246-247): dy/dx; table Leibniz / Newton / Lagrange / Euler notations; f'(a) *(unnumbered)*
  - 13.3.2 Finding f'(x) by Definition (PDF 247): Steps I-IV, ab-initio / first principles *(unnumbered)*
- 13.4 Derivation of x^n where n in Z — PDF p.250
  - (a) n positive integer via binomial theorem; (b) n negative integer; extends to rational n (PDF 250-251); Note: power rule *(unnumbered)*
- 13.5 Connection Between Derivatives and Continuity — PDF p.252
  - differentiability implies continuity, not conversely; f(x) = |x| at x = 0, Figure 13.3 (PDF 252) *(unnumbered)*

  - 1. d/dx(c) = 0; 2. power rule (PDF 253) *(unnumbered)*

#### Ch 14 — Vectors in Space
PDF 264–296 · printed 259–291 · SLOs No SLO codes printed; INTRODUCTION paragraph (PDF 264)

*Recalls plane vectors, then builds vectors in 3-D space: rectangular coordinates and the right-hand rule, components with unit vectors i, j, k, magnitude, unit vectors, distance formula, direction angles and cosines. Develops the dot product (projection, orthogonality, angle, work done), the cross product (determinant formula, parallel vectors, area of parallelogram/triangle, moment of force) and the scalar triple product (volume of parallelepiped and tetrahedron, coplanarity), with trigonometric proofs and real-world applications (torque, force magnitude, revenue).*
